the Thing given, or elſe nothing ſhall paſs by the Grant:

Ivery and Seiſin is a certain Cæremony in our Law, 
L uſes in the conveying of Lands, Tenements o- 
er Things cor 1, by Feoffment from one Mun to 
another, either in Fee- im le, Fee-tail, or for Term of 
Life, It is a Teſtimonial of that ng opening of 
him which makes the Livery, from the Thing whereof 
Livery is made. And it was ordained at firſt, that the 
common People might thereby have Notice of the Al- 
teration and g of Eftates: That they might ſure- - 
ly know in whom the Right thereof for 
Gar own Peace and Quietneſs : Perkins 209, 210. 5 
lib, 2. c. 18. ſeck. 12, The uſual manner of Delivery of 
Seiſin of Houſes, Lands, Tenements, Sc. is thus: The 
Feoftor and Feoffee (if they be preſent) or in their Ab- 
ſence, their Attornies (ſufficiently authorized in Wri-. 

ting) do come to the Houſe or Place whereof ſuch 
Seiſin is to be delivered, and there in tho Preſence of 
ſundry good Witneſſes, declare the Cauſe of their 
meeting there, and then openly-read, or cauſe to be 
read, the Deed of Feoffment, (and Letter of Attorney, 
if by Attorney) or to declare the very Effe thereof 
before them in Engliſh; which ſo done, the Feof- 
for or his Attorney taketh a Clod of Earth, ora Bough 
or Twig of a Tree thereupon growing, the Ring or 
Haſp of the Poor of an Houſe, and delivers the ſame 
with the ſaid Deed unto the Feoffee or his Attorney, 
ſaying, I deliver theſe unto you in the Name of Poſſeſſion ayd 
Seifin of all the Lands, Tenements, &c. contained in this. 
Deed, To have and to bold, according to the Form and Effe# 
ms ſame Deed; And if the Feoffment be without 
(as it may as well be) then at the Time of Deli- 
very of Seiſin, the Party muſt declare by Word of 
Mouth before Witneſſes, the very Eſtate which the 
Feoffee mult have thereby, and then delivereth Seiſin 
and Poſſe ſſion in manner aforeſaid, and then the Date 


and Manner of Seifin muſt be endorſed, >
